Picture of actress Dafne Keen in 2019

Picture of actress Jennifer Garner in 2024

Picture of actor Wesley Snipes in 2018

Picture of actor Chris Evans in 2023

Picture of actor Channing Tatum in 2015

Actors reprising their roles from earlier Marvel films include Aaron Stanford as Pyro, Dafne Keen as Laura, Jennifer Garner as Elektra, Wesley Snipes as Blade, and Chris Evans as Johnny Storm. Channing Tatum appears as Gambit, the character he was attached to play in an unproduced film.

A tribute to Fox's Marvel films is shown during the credits of *Deadpool & Wolverine*, celebrating and farewelling those films.[^cite_note-CreditsTribute-207] Levy said key themes in the film were legacy and characters' endings,[^cite_note-LevyAug2024Variety-187] which led to a "who's who of discarded Marvel characters" being included:[^cite_note-CreditsTribute-207] Garner as Elektra, [Wesley Snipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Snipes) as [Eric Brooks / Blade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_\(New_Line_franchise_character\)) from [New Line Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Line_Cinema)'s [*Blade* film trilogy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_\(franchise\)) (1998–2004), [Chris Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Evans_\(actor\)) as [Johnny Storm / Human Torch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Storm_\(film_character\)) from Fox's *[Fantastic Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantastic_Four_\(2005_film\))* (2005) and *[Fantastic Four: Rise of the Silver Surfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantastic_Four:_Rise_of_the_Silver_Surfer)* (2007), and [Channing Tatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channing_Tatum) as [Remy LeBeau / Gambit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remy_LeBeau_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)), who he was attached to star as in [an unproduced *Gambit* film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gambit_\(unproduced_film\)) for Fox.[^cite_note-NerdistVoidCameos-26] The filmmakers felt Snipes did not get an appropriate ending as Blade. Snipes was surprised to be contacted, believing he would not play the character again after Marvel cast [Mahershala Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahershala_Ali) as [Blade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) for the MCU. He and Reynolds had not kept in contact since they starred together in *[Blade: Trinity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade:_Trinity)* (2004) and Snipes struggled with Reynolds's humor on that film. Snipes said he finally understood Reynolds's approach within the context of *Deadpool & Wolverine* which made it an enjoyable experience.[^cite_note-SnipesEW-208][^cite_note-LevyAug2024Variety-187] A new costume was designed because Marvel was legally unable to use the character's signature black leather coat from New Line's films.[^cite_note-ChurchyardMPA-209] Evans quickly agreed to reprise his role due to Reynolds's involvement and humor.[^cite_note-EvansEW-210] Evans portrayed [Steve Rogers / Captain America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Rogers_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) in the MCU from 2011 to 2019, and his introduction in the film plays into this by leading the audience to believe that he is reprising his role as Rogers before revealing him to be Johnny instead.[^cite_note-ReeseWernickAug2024IW-205] Evans's costume includes elements of his Captain America and Human Torch suits from previous films.[^cite_note-ChurchyardMPA-209] For Evans's expletive-filled monologue in the [post-credits scene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-credits_scene), Reynolds offered to use [cue cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cue_cards) but Evans was excited to memorize the dialogue.[^cite_note-EvansMonologue-211] Levy was one of the directors Tatum approached for his unrealized *Gambit* film and knew it would be "deeply gratifying" for the actor to play Gambit in this film.[^cite_note-LevyAug2024Variety-187] Tatum was appreciative of efforts to include him after believing he would never have the chance to portray the character.[^cite_note-Tatum-212] A scene was shot showing Tatum's Gambit surviving the events of the film. It can be seen on the TVA monitors in the background of the post-credits scene, and Reynolds released a version of it online as a tease for the character's potential return.[^cite_note-GambitScene-213][^cite_note-GambitScene2-214]

Other characters from previous Marvel properties that were considered include Ben Affleck as [Matt Murdock / Daredevil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daredevil_\(Marvel_Comics_character\)) from *Daredevil* and *Elektra*;[^cite_note-LevyAug2024Variety-187] [Nicolas Cage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Cage) as [Johnny Blaze / Ghost Rider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_Rider_\(Johnny_Blaze\)) from the [Columbia Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_Pictures) films *[Ghost Rider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_Rider_\(2007_film\))* (2007) and *[Ghost Rider: Spirit of Vengeance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_Rider:_Spirit_of_Vengeance)* (2011);[^cite_note-Cage-215] and the main actors from the 2015 *[Fantastic Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantastic_Four_\(2015_film\))* film—[Miles Teller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miles_Teller) as [Reed Richards / Mister Fantastic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mister_Fantastic), [Kate Mara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Mara) as [Sue Storm / Invisible Woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invisible_Woman), [Michael B. Jordan](2310146.md) as [Johnny Storm / Human Torch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Torch), and [Jamie Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Bell) as [Ben Grimm / The Thing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thing_\(Marvel_Comics\)). It was decided that the latter group would not have the same impact as an appearance by Evans.[^cite_note-Fant4stic-216] The writers also considered bringing back Brolin as Cable, but decided against it because the film pays tribute to characters who "didn't get their proper ending at Fox" and Cable had only just been introduced in *Deadpool 2*. Wernick expected that Cable would go on to appear in future Marvel projects.[^cite_note-ReeseWernickAug2024IW-205]

[Swen Gillberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swen_Gillberg) was the visual effects supervisor, with effects provided by [Industrial Light & Magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Light_&_Magic) (ILM), [Framestore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framestore), [Base FX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Base_FX), Barnstorm VFX, Raynault VFX, [Rising Sun Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rising_Sun_Pictures), and [Wētā FX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C4%93t%C4%81_FX).[^cite_note-ArtOfVFX-217] Framestore joined during pre-production to work on [previsualization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Previsualization), and went on to deliver 420 final shots. The company worked on: the fight with an [army of Deadpool variants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadpool_Corps_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)), which is made to look like a [single shot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_take), including environment work and crowd duplication; the final confrontation with Cassandra and the Time Ripper machine, using the same "spaghettification" effect Framestore developed for the [second season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loki_season_2) of *Loki* (2023); and shots of Dogpool, including giving Peggy digital goggles that create a [googly eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Googly_eyes) look.[^cite_note-Framestorefxguide-218][^cite_note-Framestoreb\&a-219] ILM worked on 614 shots, primarily for scenes in the [Void](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Void_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe_location\)). Because of the filming hiatus, some scenes were filmed in Summer and others in Winter; visual effects were used to help create a consistent Winter look. The set for Cassandra's arena was extended to be made from a giant [Ant-Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Lang_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) corpse. For Johnny's death, in which Cassandra magically pulls off his skin and his body parts collapse, ILM looked at *[Bodies: The Exhibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodies:_The_Exhibition)* as part of their research. The company added a comedic moment of Johnny blinking before he collapses, to make the death more "cartoony".[^cite_note-ILM-220][^cite_note-ILMIndieWire-221] Framestore and ILM both worked on Cassandra's powers, which involve her hands moving through other characters' faces. Gillberg was inspired for this by comic book art. Corrin was filmed hiding their arm so it could be replaced with a digital arm that interacts with a [digital double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_double) of the other actor.[^cite_note-Gillbergb\&a-222] Wētā returned from the first two *Deadpool* films to provide facial animation for Deadpool's mask, again using a 2D effect to morph the mask. Reynolds provided reference footage for this, and a new [machine learning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_learning) tool was developed to suggest facial movements based on his dialogue. During production, Wētā's work expanded to around 630 shots, including animation for Wolverine's mask; augmenting the environment and Wolverine's skeleton for the opening sequence; shots of the TVA and their technology, based on the effects seen in *Loki*; and environment work for other parts of the Void.[^cite_note-WetaAM-223][^cite_note-Wetafxguide-224][^cite_note-WetaAWN-225] Blood and gore was added digitally.[^cite_note-Framestoreb\&a-219][^cite_note-ILMIndieWire-221] [Method Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Method_Studios), returning from their work on *Deadpool 2*'s opening sequence, worked with the other vendors to plan *Deadpool & Wolverine*'s opening sequence and add the title cards.[^cite_note-MethodStudios-226]

## Music

Main article: [*Deadpool & Wolverine* (soundtrack)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadpool_&_Wolverine_\(soundtrack\))

[Rob Simonsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rob_Simonsen) was hired to compose the score by mid-July 2023. He previously worked with Levy on *The Adam Project* and the [fourth season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stranger_Things_season_4) of *Stranger Things*.[^cite_note-Simonsen-227] A soundtrack album for the film was released digitally by [Hollywood Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Records) on July 24, 2024, with a physical release on July 26. It includes 17 songs featured in, or inspired by, the film along with "LFG" which is Simonsen's main theme for *Deadpool & Wolverine*.[^cite_note-Soundtrack-228][^cite_note-Soundtrack2-229] An album featuring Simonsen's score was released digitally on July 24 with a regular version and a deluxe edition, the latter also including the songs from the soundtrack album.[^cite_note-Soundtrack-228] An [EP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extended_play), titled *Deadpool & Wolverine: Madonna's "Like a Prayer" EP*, featuring "Like a Prayer" by Madonna as well as remixed versions of the song featured in the film, was released by [Warner Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Records) on August 9.[^cite_note-LikeAPrayer-230]

## Marketing

A teaser trailer was released during [Super Bowl LVIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LVIII) in February 2024. Nick Romano from *[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly)* said it confirmed the film's R rating and the fact that Deadpool would be "infiltrating" the MCU.[^cite_note-EWSuperBowl-113] Austin Goslin of *[Polygon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygon_\(website\))* also highlighted the use of MCU elements and questioned whether Reynolds was playing the same version of Deadpool as he did in the previous films.[^cite_note-PolygonSuperBowl-36] *[Wired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wired_\(magazine\))*'s Angela Wattercutter noted a sexually explicit [pegging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegging_\(sexual_practice\)) joke, a first for the MCU, and speculated about queer themes that the film could explore further than previous MCU projects.[^cite_note-WiredSuperBowl-231] The trailer's inclusion of the [*Secret Wars* (2015) #5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secret_Wars_\(2015_comic_book\)#Issue_five) comic book issue was also discussed, given that Marvel Studios had announced the film *[Avengers: Secret Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers:_Secret_Wars)* (2027).[^cite_note-PolygonTeaserEEs-232][^cite_note-CBRTeaserSWComic-233][^cite_note-TotalFilmTeaserEEs-234] Shortly after the trailer's premiere, Jackman posted a tongue-in-cheek jab at Reynolds with a modified logo displaying "*Wolverine & Asshole*".[^cite_note-WolverineAsshole-235] The trailer had 365 million global views in its first 24 hours, including the 123 million Super Bowl viewers who saw a 30-second version, surpassing *No Way Home*'s record (355.5 million views) to become the most-viewed trailer in that time period.[^cite_note-TrailerViews-101][^cite_note-TrailerViews2-236] Reynolds and Jackman were presented with [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records) certificates commemorating the achievement during an interview on [ITV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITV_\(TV_Network\))'s *[This Morning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Morning_\(TV_programme\))*.[^cite_note-365Record-237]

Picture of director Shawn Levy in 2024

Director Shawn Levy at CinemaCon in April 2024, where he promoted the film

A "silence your phones" [public service announcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_service_announcement) (PSA) with Deadpool and Wolverine opened Disney's presentation at [CinemaCon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CinemaCon) in April 2024, during which Feige and Levy debuted nine minutes of footage from the film. *[The Hollywood Reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hollywood_Reporter)* said this "drew the biggest laughter" of all the presentations.[^cite_note-2024CinemaCon-238] It included repurposed *[Thor: The Dark World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thor:_The_Dark_World)* (2013) footage of [Chris Hemsworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Hemsworth) as [Thor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thor_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)), who is mourning the supposed death of Deadpool rather than [Loki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loki_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) as in that film.[^cite_note-HemsworthArchiveFootage-57][^cite_note-HemsworthInterview-58] The PSA was later released publicly.[^cite_note-PSARelease-239] An official trailer set to "Like a Prayer" by Madonna debuted on April 22.[^cite_note-OfficialTrailer-240] Another trailer, released on May 20, announced ticket pre-sales. It included a [QR code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QR_code) that led to a video of Reynolds reading a disclaimer for the film.[^cite_note-QRCodeDisclaimer-241] At the end of May, a special popcorn bucket for the theatrical release was revealed,[^cite_note-PopcornBucketReveal-242] after Feige had said it would be "intentionally crude and lewd". This was inspired by the [viral popcorn bucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dune:_Part_Two#Popcorn_bucket_meme) for *[Dune: Part Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dune:_Part_Two)* (2024).[^cite_note-CrudePopcornBucket-243] The bucket is shaped like Wolverine's head with a "comically oversized" mouth for the popcorn or other snacks. A video reveal, set to the "[Also sprach Zarathustra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Also_sprach_Zarathustra)" fanfare which was famously used in *[2001: A Space Odyssey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001:_A_Space_Odyssey)* (1968), shows Deadpool caressing the bucket as popcorn falls into its mouth and butter drips down its nose.[^cite_note-PopcornBucketReveal-242]

A teaser released in early June revealed that the character [Ladypool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Deadpool_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) would appear, prompting speculation about who played the role,[^cite_note-Ladypool-244] subsequently revealed to be Lively.[^cite_note-LivelyLadypool-47] Later that month, Reynolds appeared as Deadpool in a "typically irreverent video" at [CineEurope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CineEurope) to introduce 12 minutes of footage from the film.[^cite_note-CineEurope-245] The cast went on a global marketing tour starting in early July, stopping at: [Shanghai](27643.md), where Reynolds, Jackman, and Levy attended a fan event to screen 35 minutes of the film;[^cite_note-Deadline3WeeksBOTracking-246][^cite_note-CNNMarketingTour-247][^cite_note-ShanghaiTourFootage-248] [Seoul](19159283.md), where the actors appeared at the 2024 [Waterbomb festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterbomb_festival);[^cite_note-SeoulMarketingTour-249] [Berlin](3354.md), where the trio were joined by Corrin and attended the [UEFA Euro 2024 quarterfinals match between Netherlands and Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024_knockout_stage#Netherlands_vs_Turkey);[^cite_note-CNNMarketingTour-247][^cite_note-CorrinBerlinTour-250][^cite_note-BerlinTourEuroMatch-251] London, where the four actors and Levy were joined by Delaney, dog actor Peggy, and executive producers Louis D'Esposito and Wendy Jacobson;[^cite_note-LondonTourBleedingCool-252][^cite_note-LondonTourHMVLogo-253] and [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md), where they visited [Maracanã Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maracan%C3%A3_Stadium).[^cite_note-BrazilTour-254] Coinciding with the London visit, United Kingdom music and entertainment retailer [HMV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMV) temporarily altered their logo at one of their stores to feature Dogpool instead of the dog [Nipper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nipper).[^cite_note-LondonTourHMVLogo-253]

Deadpool and Wolverine appeared in an ad for *[The Bachelorette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bachelorette_\(American_TV_series\))* on July 8 during the [season 21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bachelorette_\(American_TV_series\)_season_21) premiere, to entice the [Bachelor Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_Nation) audience (the 18–49 female demographic) to see the film. The ad was from Maximum Effort, Loon Productions, and Really Original, and was directed by Oren Brimer. Reynolds also discussed the premiere episode on social media.[^cite_note-BacheloretteAd-255] On July 19, Deadpool and Wolverine appeared in a music video for the single "[Chk Chk Boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chk_Chk_Boom)" by South Korean boy band [Stray Kids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stray_Kids), whose song "[Slash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slash_\(song\))" is on the soundtrack.[^cite_note-ChkChkBoom-256] For the [Formula One](10854.md) [2024 Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Belgian_Grand_Prix), the [Alpine F1 Team](65240620.md)—of which Reynolds is an investor—revealed Deadpool and Wolverine livery on July 24, which they raced in to promote the film.[^cite_note-DP\&WRacecar-257] On July 25, Reynolds, Jackman, Levy, Corrin, and Feige screened the film at [San Diego Comic-Con](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Comic-Con). This included a video introduction from Uggams as Blind Al, and concluded with Reynolds bringing out the major cameo actors: Keen, Garner, Evans, Tatum, and Snipes. Following the panel, a drone show was held at [Petco Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petco_Park) that included images of Deadpool and Wolverine.[^cite_note-SDCC2024Panel-258] The July 25 episode of the television game show *[Press Your Luck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Your_Luck#2019_revival)* featured Deadpool and Wolverine taking over the Whammy, the show's mascot.[^cite_note-LuckShow-259] The week after the film's release, [NSYNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSYNC) altered the title for the official music video of their song "[Bye Bye Bye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bye_Bye_Bye)" to say "\*NSYNC – Bye Bye Bye (Official Video from *Deadpool and Wolverine*)" after the song's use in the opening credits boosted its popularity.[^cite_note-ByeByeBye-260]

Disney spent \$135 million to promote the film,[^cite_note-DeadlineMonRecord-261] working with Maximum Effort and brand partners such as:[^cite_note-MarketingDive-262] Reynolds's [Aviation Gin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviation_Gin),[^cite_note-AviationGin-263][^cite_note-AviationGin2-264] [Heineken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heineken),[^cite_note-Heineken-265] [DiGiorno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DiGiorno),[^cite_note-DiGiorno-266] [Jack in the Box](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_in_the_Box),[^cite_note-JackBox-267] [Heinz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinz),[^cite_note-Heinz-268] [Tim Hortons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Hortons),[^cite_note-Café-269] SuperX,[^cite_note-SuperX-270] [Hot Topic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Topic),[^cite_note-HotTopic-271] Homage,[^cite_note-HomageShirts-272] [Xbox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xbox),[^cite_note-Xbox-273] [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas),[^cite_note-Adidas-274] [Casio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casio),[^cite_note-Casio-275] [Dave & Buster's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_&_Buster's), [Best Friends Animal Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Friends_Animal_Society), [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola), [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_\(operating_system\)), [Old Spice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Spice), [Turner Classic Movies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turner_Classic_Movies), and [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic).[^cite_note-DeadlineMonRecord-261][^cite_note-THRMarketing-276] [Funko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funko) and [Hot Toys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Toys) produced action figures based on the film,[^cite_note-FunkoDuo-277][^cite_note-FunkoGroup-278][^cite_note-HotToys-279] and Funko allowed attendees at the 2024 San Diego Comic-Con to create personalized versions of their Deadpool and Wolverine [Funko Pop!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funko_Pop!) figures.[^cite_note-FunkoPopYourselfSDCC-280] [Variant covers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variant_cover) for Marvel Comics issues featuring production stills and promotional images from the film were released in August,[^cite_note-VariantCovers-281] and more variant covers featuring concept art from the film debuted in December.[^cite_note-VariantCovers2-282]

## Release

### Theatrical

*Deadpool & Wolverine* premiered at the [David H. Koch Theater](1587430.md) in New York City on July 22, 2024.[^cite_note-Premiere-283] It was released in France, Germany, Italy, South Korea, and Japan on July 24; in Spain, Brazil, the United Kingdom, Mexico, and Australia on July 25;[^cite_note-DeadlineOpeningBOTracking-284] and in the United States and China on July 26,[^cite_note-Jul2024Release-169][^cite_note-ChinaRelease-285] in [IMAX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMAX), [RealD 3D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RealD_3D), [Dolby Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolby_Cinema), [4DX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4DX), [Cinemark XD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinemark_Theatres), and other [premium large formats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premium_large_format) (PLFs).[^cite_note-DPWolverineMarvel-105] The film was originally announced with a release date of September 6, 2024,[^cite_note-ReynoldsJackmanRelease-17] before it was pushed back to November 8 to accommodate other MCU film delays.[^cite_note-Nov2024Release-118] Its release was then moved forward to May 3 of that year when Disney adjusted its release calendar due to the WGA strike,[^cite_note-May2024Release-154] before settling on the July 2024 date after the SAG-AFTRA strike ended.[^cite_note-Jul2024Release-169] The film is part of [Phase Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Cinematic_Universe:_Phase_Five) of the MCU.[^cite_note-May2024Release-154] The [Motion Picture Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_Picture_Association) gave it an R rating, a first for the MCU.[^cite_note-OfficialRRating-286] In August, Levy said there would not be a [director's cut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director's_cut) because the theatrical version was exactly the film he wanted to make. Reynolds said he previously discussed a director's cut with Levy, who refused.[^cite_note-NoExtendedCut-287]

### Home media

The film was released for [digital download](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_distribution) on October 1, 2024, and on [Ultra HD Blu-ray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultra_HD_Blu-ray), [Blu-ray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blu-ray), and [DVD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DVD-Video) on October 22. The home media releases include deleted scenes, a gag reel, commentary by Levy and Reynolds, and featurettes focused on the "Like a Prayer" fight scene, the major cameos, Jackman's return as Wolverine, and a tribute to production designer Ray Chan following his death in April 2024.[^cite_note-HomeMedia-288] *Deadpool & Wolverine* had the biggest first week on U.S. digital platforms for an R-rated film, surpassing previous record holder *[John Wick: Chapter 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wick:_Chapter_4)* (2023).[^cite_note-DeadlineAdtlRecords-289] *Deadpool & Wolverine* was released on [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+) on November 12.[^cite_note-DisneyPlus-290] Disney said it had 19.4 million global views in its first six days on the platform, making it the most-watched live-action film on Disney+ since the release of *[Black Panther: Wakanda Forever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Panther:_Wakanda_Forever)* in early 2023.[^cite_note-DisneyViews-291] [Nielsen Media Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nielsen_Media_Research), which records streaming viewership on U.S. television screens, said it was the most-streamed film in its first two weeks on Disney+ with 1.34 billion minutes in its first week and 586 million minutes in its second.[^cite_note-NielsenNov17-292][^cite_note-NielsenNov24-293]

## Reception

### Box office

*Deadpool & Wolverine* grossed \$636.7 million in the United States and Canada, and \$701.3 million in other territories, for a worldwide total of \$1.338 billion.[^cite_note-BOM-3] The film surpassed *[Joker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joker_\(2019_film\))* (2019) to become the highest grossing R-rated film of all time, doing so in 23 days.[^cite_note-RRatingRecord-294] It also became the [fifth-highest grossing MCU film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Marvel_Cinematic_Universe_films#Box_office_performance) and the [20th-highest-grossing film of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_films) during its release.[^cite_note-DeadlineAdtlRecords-289][^cite_note-HighestGrossingPeak-295] *[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline_Hollywood)* calculated a net profit of \$400 million, the third-highest for a 2024 film, accounting for the reported \$200 million production budget, marketing costs, and other expenses against box office grosses, television and streaming, and home media revenues.[^cite_note-DeadlineProfits-296] Based on the \$429 million net budget, Caroline Reid at *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* estimated a box office profit of \$241 million. This did not take into account marketing costs or revenues from streaming, home media, and merchandise.[^cite_note-Budget-2]

[Fandango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fandango_Media) said ticket pre-sales for the film were the site's best for 2024 and any R-rated film, while [AMC Theatres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMC_Theatres) said 200,000 people bought first-day tickets which was the most sales for any R-rated film at the theater chain. Pamela McClintock of *The Hollywood Reporter* predicted the film would debut to more than \$100 million, something no 2024 film release had done by late May.[^cite_note-THRPreSaleTickets-297] Tracking services projected it would open between \$160–165 million in the U.S. and Canada,[^cite_note-Deadline3WeeksBOTracking-246] and between \$340–360 million worldwide. The latter took into consideration substantial walk-up business observed from Latino and Hispanic moviegoers for recent films.[^cite_note-DeadlineOpeningBOTracking-284]

On opening day in the U.S. and Canada, *Deadpool & Wolverine* made \$96 million including \$38.5 million from Thursday night previews. Both amounts were records for an R-rated film.[^cite_note-DeadlineOpenWknd-298][^cite_note-THRPreviews-299] The film exceeded predictions for its opening weekend, grossing \$211.4 million in the U.S. and Canada and \$444.6 million worldwide. That gave it the biggest opening of an R-rated film ever and the biggest opening of 2024, and was also the sixth-biggest opening ever for the U.S. and Canada.[^cite_note-DeadlineOpenWknd-298][^cite_note-DeadlineOpenWkndIntl-300] The film recovered its reported production budget in just two days.[^cite_note-BudgetBack-301] After three days of release, it became the highest-grossing R-rated Disney film, surpassing the studio's previous record which was set by *[Pretty Woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretty_Woman)* in 1990.[^cite_note-DeadlineOpenWknd-298] During the following week, it set the record for an R-rated film on a Monday with \$24.4 million,[^cite_note-DeadlineMonRecord-261] on a Tuesday with \$25.3 million,[^cite_note-DeadlineTueRecord-302] on a Wednesday with \$19.3 million,[^cite_note-DeadlineWedRecord-303] and on a non-preview Thursday with \$18 million.[^cite_note-DeadlineThuRecord-304] The film remained in first place for the U.S. and Canada in its second weekend with \$97 million.[^cite_note-THRWknd2-305] It crossed the \$1 billion mark worldwide in its third weekend, becoming the second film to do so in 2024.[^cite_note-DeadlineWknd3-306] Also during the third weekend, Reynolds's *Deadpool & Wolverine* and Lively's *[It Ends With Us](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/It_Ends_with_Us_\(film\))* occupied the number one and two spots at the box office, a feat that had not been accomplished for a married Hollywood couple since [Bruce Willis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Willis)'s *[Die Hard 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Hard_2)* and [Demi Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demi_Moore)'s *[Ghost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_\(1990_film\))* in 1990.[^cite_note-CoupleRecord-307] The film was surpassed at the U.S. and Canada box office in its fourth weekend by newcomer *[Alien: Romulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alien:_Romulus)*,[^cite_note-DeadlineWknd4-308] but returned to the number one spot for its fifth and sixth weekends.[^cite_note-DeadlineWknd5-309][^cite_note-THRWknd6-310] It remained in the U.S. and Canada top ten through eleven weeks, ending its run as the [second-highest-grossing film of 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_in_film#Highest-grossing_films) behind *[Inside Out 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inside_Out_2)*,[^cite_note-DeadlineAdtlRecords-289] and 20th-highest-grossing film worldwide. Its highest grossing territories outside the U.S. and Canada were the United Kingdom (\$73.8M), China (\$59.7M), Australia (\$44.5M), Mexico (\$43.5M), and Germany (\$40.7M).[^cite_note-DeadlineIntlFinish-311]

### Critical response

Picture of actors Ryan Reynolds and Hugh Jackman at San Diego Comic-Con in 2024

Ryan Reynolds and Hugh Jackman promoting the film at San Diego Comic-Con in July 2024. Both actors received positive reviews for their performances in the film.

Critics praised the performances of Reynolds and Jackman as well as the humor, but were less positive about the film overall.[^cite_note-NYTCriticalConsensus-312] On review aggregator [Rotten Tomatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotten_Tomatoes), 77% of 421 critics gave it a positive review and an average rating of 6.9/10. The website's critics consensus reads, "Ryan Reynolds makes himself at home in the MCU with acerbic wit while Hugh Jackman provides an Adamantium backbone to proceedings in *Deadpool & Wolverine*, an irreverent romp with a surprising soft spot for a bygone era of superhero movies."[^cite_note-313] [Metacritic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metacritic), which uses a [weighted average](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weighted_average), assigned a score of 56 out of 100 based on 58 critics, indicating "mixed or average" reviews.[^cite_note-314] Audiences polled by [CinemaScore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CinemaScore) gave it an average grade of "A" on an A+ to F scale, the same as the previous *Deadpool* films. Those surveyed by [PostTrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PostTrak) gave it a 96% positive score and 85% said they would definitely recommend it.[^cite_note-DeadlineOpenWknd-298]

*[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline_Hollywood)*'s Pete Hammond said the titular team-up "works *beyond* your wildest screen-team dreams" and felt Jackman gave his best performance as Wolverine in the film.[^cite_note-DeadlineReview-315] Olly Richards at *[Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_\(magazine\))* also praised Jackman's performance, saying he elevated the material in key moments and "in a film with a million dick jokes, he manages to deliver a character arc that's genuinely moving". Richards was positive about the focus on the title characters and the audience's immediate experience over wider MCU set-up.[^cite_note-EmpireReview-316] Reviewing the film for *[RogerEbert.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RogerEbert.com)*, [Matt Zoller Seitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Zoller_Seitz) gave it two-and-a-half stars out of four. He said Jackman and Reynolds both gave moving performances as "broken, abandoned men", and he especially cared about their characters during the climactic "[pathos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathos) firehose".[^cite_note-RogerEbertReview-317] Ross Bonaime at *[Collider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collider_\(website\))* said Deadpool was able to live up to his full potential with his integration into the MCU, while Jackman was able to be even more brutal than in previous films as Wolverine.[^cite_note-ColliderReview-318] At the [Associated Press](18935732.md), Krysta Fauria found the [homoerotic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homoerotic) yet hate-filled central relationship to be enticing and compelling.[^cite_note-AssociatedPressReview-319] *[San Francisco Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Chronicle)* critic [Mick LaSalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mick_LaSalle) commended the contrast between Jackman's serious performance and Reynolds's humor.[^cite_note-SFCReview-320]

Dominic Baez at *[The Seattle Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Seattle_Times)* said the film was "pure, unadulterated fun" and a love letter to Marvel fans.[^cite_note-SeattleTimesReview-321] [CNN](62028.md)'s Brian Lowry said it showcased Marvel Studios's willingness to partake in self-deprecation,[^cite_note-CNNReview-322] while *The Hollywood Reporter*'s David Rooney said the meta jokes were "dialed up to radioactive levels". He felt the humor was the "sturdiest" part of the film.[^cite_note-THRReview-323] Fauria said the increased focus on self-aware genre criticism made the film an improvement over its predecessors.[^cite_note-AssociatedPressReview-319] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* critic [Peter Bradshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Bradshaw) said Reynolds was "often funny, sometimes very funny, periodically entirely unbearable" and had lived up to Deadpool's self-claimed title of "Marvel Jesus" by "repositioning \[the MCU] as gag material and keep\[ing] the whole thing ticking over".[^cite_note-GuardianReview-324] Nick Schager of *[The Daily Beast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Beast)* praised the film as "the shot in the arm—and kick to the nuts—that's urgently needed" in the MCU, and was impressed with how the R-rated Deadpool had been integrated with the PG-13 franchise.[^cite_note-DailyBeastReview-325] Writing for *[Vanity Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_Fair_\(magazine\))*, [Richard Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Lawson_\(writer\)) similarly felt it "somehow kind of works", despite the humor focusing on entertainment industry details.[^cite_note-VanityFairReview-326] Katie Walsh at the *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* disagreed with this point, saying the "quips, references, fourth-wall breaking, celebrity gossip, Hollywood inside baseball, jabs at other film studios, \[and] ironically retro needle drops" were only for fans and the "terminally online".[^cite_note-LATReview-327] Tom Jorgensen of *[IGN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IGN)* found it to be hilarious and highlighted the jokes related to the MCU and Feige, but felt there were some "pulled punches" compared to some of the "heinous on-screen acts" in the previous films.[^cite_note-IGNReview-328] In contrast, Donald Clarke for *[The Irish Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irish_Times)* was critical of the juvenile humor and scored the film just one star out of five.[^cite_note-IrishTimesReview-329]

Writing for *Variety*, Peter Debruge said the film was an "irreverent send-off" to Fox's Marvel films that worked better as an homage to the past than as a model for future films. He said Levy was stronger at comedy than action, with the latter not holding up to Leitch's work on *Deadpool 2*.[^cite_note-VarietyReview-176] [Caryn James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caryn_James) at the [BBC](19344654.md) and [Justin Chang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Chang) of [NPR](29697232.md) also criticized Levy's work on the action sequences.[^cite_note-BBCReview-330][^cite_note-NPRReview-331] Chang described it as a "self-cannibalizing slog" with a derivative storyline, and said it was undermining its satire of the MCU by being an MCU film itself.[^cite_note-NPRReview-331] Alissa Wilkinson at *[The New York Times](30680.md)* had similar thoughts about the film making jokes about Disney, who the audience pays to watch the film.[^cite_note-NYTReview-332] [Jordan Hoffman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Hoffman) of *[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly)* scored it a "C-" and called it "too repetitive, too immature, and too irritating to enjoy" with no stakes or drama.[^cite_note-EWReview-333] *[Chicago Sun-Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sun-Times)* columnist [Richard Roeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Roeper), who gave a more positive review with three stars out of four, highlighted the lead performances but criticized the paper-thin plot and obligatory villains.[^cite_note-ChicagoSTReview-334] Rooney described the plot as "a lumpy stew of familiar elements, given minimal narrative clarity" despite a large amount of exposition, and found the overall film to be "messy and overstuffed" even though he thought it would be a crowd pleaser.[^cite_note-THRReview-323] For *[Vulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulture_\(website\))*, [Bilge Ebiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilge_Ebiri) wrote: "*Deadpool & Wolverine* isn't a particularly good movie—I'm not even sure it is a movie—but it's so determined to beat you down with its incessant irreverence that you might find yourself submitting to it."[^cite_note-VultureReview-335]

## Documentary special

Main article: [*Marvel Studios: Assembled*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Studios:_Assembled)

In February 2021, the documentary series *Marvel Studios: Assembled* was announced.[^cite_note-Assembled-360] The special on this film, "[The Making of *Deadpool & Wolverine*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Making_of_Deadpool_&_Wolverine)", was released on Disney+ on November 12, 2024.[^cite_note-AssembledRelease-361]

## Future

With the film's release, Reynolds said he was unsure when he would next portray Deadpool.[^cite_note-ReynoldsLevyAug2024Collider-85] Discussing the [flashforward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flashforward) that Deadpool sees in the film, in which a dying Deadpool is held by a crying Thor, Reese said it may be ignored moving forward but he expected that the MCU would consider it canonical and work towards that scene appearing in full during a future project.[^cite_note-ReeseWernickAug2024IW-205] In November 2024, Feige said the studio was looking for ways to include Deadpool and Wolverine in future MCU projects.[^cite_note-FeigeNov2024-362] The next month, Reynolds said he had some ideas for the character's future. He was interested in Deadpool appearing alongside the [Avengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) or the X-Men, but he did not want the character to join either of those teams because that would be "the end of his journey". He felt Deadpool worked well in a supporting or ensemble role and wanted to keep the character paired with Wolverine for future appearances. Reynolds was reluctant to feature Deadpool as the lead character of a film again, saying that "works best if you take everything away from him and put his back against the wall. I can't really do that again. A fourth time feels a little iterative and redundant."[^cite_note-ReynoldsDec2024-363] In April 2025, Reynolds said he was writing an ensemble project,[^cite_note-ReynoldsApr2025-364] which was revealed the following month to be various script treatments for an ensemble film centered on three or four X-Men characters alongside Deadpool. *[The Hollywood Reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hollywood_Reporter)* reported that Reynolds was working independently of Marvel Studios to figure out the film's concept before taking his ideas to them.[^cite_note-ReynoldsDeadpoolXMenTeamUp-365] In July 2025, Tatiana Siegel of *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(magazine\))* reported that Marvel Studios executives did not feel a sense of urgency in making a fourth *Deadpool* film amidst a wider "pullback" of the superhero genre.[^cite_note-VarietyNextMovesJul2025-366]

In January 2026, [Kim Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Masters) of *[Puck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puck_\(media_company\))* reported that Reynolds was focused on making the next *Deadpool* film.[^cite_note-ReynoldsFocusJan2026-367] In April, Reynolds said he had "some stuff kind of written" and reiterated that he wanted Deadpool to be a supporting character and part of a group of characters for the next film.[^cite_note-ReynoldsApr2026-368] Reynolds stated in July that another *Deadpool* film would be happening "eventually". He felt there were some "deep cuts" from the comics that had not been explored in the previous films, specifically highlighting the comics written by [Fabian Nicieza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabian_Nicieza) and [Gerry Duggan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerry_Duggan_\(writer\)). Justin Kroll at *[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline_Hollywood)* reported that the next *Deadpool* film was in early development and had no writer or director attached.[^cite_note-ReynoldsJul2026FantasticFest-369]

Most critics have found something to like in *Deadpool & Wolverine*... They say it has a superpowered jokes-per-minute ratio and two winning performances from its lead actors. But many reviewers had a mixed-to-negative assessment of the movie overall, calling it difficult to follow, lacking in real tension or stakes and overly reliant on self-referential story lines.
